Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I, um... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. It feels a little silly to even talk about, especially at my age, but... well, I work in production, and recently they asked me to start helping out with the safety trainings. Which is a good thing! 

But... whenever I'm up there pointing things out to the new hires, I just... I can’t seem to stop noticing how wrinkly and, I don't know, uneven my hands look. It's a little embarrassing to admit I care about that. And then they put some photos of the trainings in the employee newsletter, and now I just keep thinking my face looks completely crooked in them. The other ladies at work say they don’t see it at all, so I know I shouldn't make a fuss... but it's just been bothering me a lot lately.

Well, um... I suppose I do think about it a bit more than I'd like to admit. I try not to let it distract me, I really do! 

But like I was saying before, ever since I started helping with those safety trainings, it just sort of stays on my mind. I'll be up there pointing at the equipment, and I just can't seem to stop noticing how wrinkly and uneven my hands look. And then I'll go back to the line, and the thought just kind of follows me. Then, oh goodness, ever since they put those photos in the newsletter, the idea that my face looks all crooked just keeps popping into my head while I'm trying to work.

Oh, um... no, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing anything important. I'm still at work every day, and I still do the safety trainings, of course. I wouldn't let anyone down like that.

And, um, I guess I have been sort of... stepping out of the way when someone brings out a camera at work, or maybe pausing for a second before saying yes to going out with the girls after our shift. Just little things, really! I still go, mostly. I just don't want to ruin anyone's pictures or make them uncomfortable if I'm looking a bit... off. 

It's really nothing major, I promise. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.

Oh, um... that's a really good question. I mean... when I look down at my hands, or when I see myself in the mirror, I really do see the wrinkles and the unevenness. It looks very real to me in the moment. 

But... well, since everyone else tells me they don't see what I'm seeing, I try very hard to tell myself that maybe it is just my imagination acting up. I certainly don't want to seem like I've lost my senses or anything! So I guess... I guess I feel like they look that way, but I'm trying to accept that I'm probably just being overly critical. I try to trust what other people say more than my own eyes, if that makes sense? 

I really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous saying that. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?

Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that.

Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that. \n\nSometimes I might feel a little self-conscious, like I mentioned before, but I don't actually think *they* are staring at my hands or thinking bad things about my face. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 64 / production_worker / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors\nmanifestation: \"Before a class or a senior-center workshop, I’ll check the bathroom mirror a few times, put lotion on my hands, turn my face different ways, and sometimes ask a coworker if I look all right, which makes me run a little behind but I still get myself there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. 

If I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. I'll look in the mirror a few times, maybe turn my face this way and that, just to see how it looks in the light. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. 

Sometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. 

I just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient experiencing Body Dysmorphic Disorder, in a diagnostic interview with a\npsychiatrist. I really don't think that. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together.

I'm sorry to repeat myself, I just... I hope I'm explaining it right. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. 

I suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? 

And maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! 

It's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. 

Sometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?

Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. \n\nIf I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. \n\nI suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? \n\nAnd maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your reflection or groom yourself at specific times of the day or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! \n\nIt's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. \n\nSometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything drastic like that. I'm still a very happy person, mostly! I mean, it hasn't stopped me from going to the plant or doing my volunteer work, and I still love doing those things.

Oh, well... I mean, I try not to let my mind wander too much when I'm just going about my day. I like to stay focused on my work, you know? 

But, um... I guess if I'm being perfectly honest... yes, they do pop into my head sometimes. It really all started when I began helping out with those safety trainings at the plant. I'd be up there, pointing things out on the diagrams, and I just couldn't help but notice how... well, how wrinkly and uneven my hands looked. And then, once that started, I began noticing my face more, especially in the company newsletter photos. I just keep thinking my face looks sort of... crooked? 

Even when I'm not up there teaching or looking at a picture, I'll just be sitting on the assembly line and suddenly remember how my hands looked pointing at the board. Or I'll wonder if my face looked uneven when I was talking to my supervisor. I know it sounds a bit silly, because the other ladies say they don't see what I'm talking about at all. I shouldn't be dwelling on it so much, should I? I just want to make sure I'm presenting a good image for the company. That's a good thing to care about, right?

Then, oh goodness, ever since they put those photos in the newsletter, the idea that my face looks all crooked just keeps popping into my head while I'm trying to work. \n\nI try to push it away, especially since my coworkers tell me they don't see it at all, so I know it's just me being foolish... but it does keep coming back to mind throughout my shift. I'm sorry to repeat myself, I just... I hope I'm explaining it right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has thinking about your hands and face been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say \"distress,\" exactly. That sounds so... so dramatic, you know? I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. It's more just... a little bothersome. \n\nI mean, I still get my work done, and I'm still smiling and chatting with the girls on the line. It's just a quiet little worry in the back of my head that I wish I could turn off. I feel a bit silly letting it get under my skin at all, to be perfectly honest. I know I should just count my blessings and ignore it. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just don't want you to think I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your appearance interfered with your daily activities or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing anything important. I'm still at work every day, and I still do the safety trainings, of course. I wouldn't let anyone down like that. \n\nI mean, I suppose... well, I might be taking just a tiny bit longer to get ready in the mornings before a training day, just trying to make sure I look presentable. And, um, I guess I have been sort of... stepping out of the way when someone brings out a camera at work, or maybe pausing for a second before saying yes to going out with the girls after our shift. Just little things, really! I still go, mostly. I just don't want to ruin anyone's pictures or make them uncomfortable if I'm looking a bit... off. \n\nIt's really nothing major, I promise. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your hands and face actually look uneven and wrinkly, or do you just feel like they might?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a really good question. I mean... when I look down at my hands, or when I see myself in the mirror, I really do see the wrinkles and the unevenness. It looks very real to me in the moment. \n\nBut... well, since everyone else tells me they don't see what I'm seeing, I try very hard to tell myself that maybe it is just my imagination acting up. I certainly don't want to seem like I've lost my senses or anything! So I guess... I guess I feel like they look that way, but I'm trying to accept that I'm probably just being overly critical. I try to trust what other people say more than my own eyes, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous saying that. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that. \n\nSometimes I might feel a little self-conscious, like I mentioned before, but I don't actually think *they* are staring at my hands or thinking bad things about my face. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. \n\nIf I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. I'll look in the mirror a few times, maybe turn my face this way and that, just to see how it looks in the light. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. \n\nI just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. \n\nI suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? \n\nAnd maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your reflection or groom yourself at specific times of the day or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! \n\nIt's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. \n\nSometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your concerns about your appearance are affecting your overall happiness or well-being?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything drastic like that. I'm still a very happy person, mostly! I mean, it hasn't stopped me from going to the plant or doing my volunteer work, and I still love doing those things. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe it takes a little bit of the joy out of things sometimes? Just a smidge. Like I said, I'm a bit slower getting ready for my trainings because I'm fussing over how I look, and... and I do get a little anxious before social events now. I used to just breeze right into parties and get-togethers without a second thought, and now I hesitate. And, um, dodging the cameras when people are taking photos... it does make me feel a little silly, if I'm being honest. \n\nBut I'm still getting out there and living my life, you know? It's just a minor bump in the road. I'm sure lots of women my age feel a little less confident sometimes, right? It's perfectly normal to not be thrilled with getting older, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your hands and face when you're not looking at them or getting ready to be in front of people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I mean, I try not to let my mind wander too much when I'm just going about my day. I like to stay focused on my work, you know? \n\nBut, um... I guess if I'm being perfectly honest... yes, they do pop into my head sometimes. It really all started when I began helping out with those safety trainings at the plant. I'd be up there, pointing things out on the diagrams, and I just couldn't help but notice how... well, how wrinkly and uneven my hands looked. And then, once that started, I began noticing my face more, especially in the company newsletter photos. I just keep thinking my face looks sort of... crooked? \n\nEven when I'm not up there teaching or looking at a picture, I'll just be sitting on the assembly line and suddenly remember how my hands looked pointing at the board. Or I'll wonder if my face looked uneven when I was talking to my supervisor. I know it sounds a bit silly, because the other ladies say they don't see what I'm talking about at all. I shouldn't be dwelling on it so much, should I? I just want to make sure I'm presenting a good image for the company. That's a good thing to care about, right?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Oh, my family and friends are just wonderful. I really try my best not to let it cause any real problems with them. Like I was saying a little bit ago, it certainly hasn't stopped me from working or doing my volunteering, and I still see everyone! 

But, um... I guess it does change things just a tiny bit when we're together. I just don't want you to think I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your appearance interfered with your daily activities or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing anything important. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? It looks very real to me in the moment. \n\nBut... well, since everyone else tells me they don't see what I'm seeing, I try very hard to tell myself that maybe it is just my imagination acting up. I certainly don't want to seem like I've lost my senses or anything! So I guess... I guess I feel like they look that way, but I'm trying to accept that I'm probably just being overly critical. I try to trust what other people say more than my own eyes, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous saying that. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that. \n\nSometimes I might feel a little self-conscious, like I mentioned before, but I don't actually think *they* are staring at my hands or thinking bad things about my face. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. \n\nIf I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. I'll look in the mirror a few times, maybe turn my face this way and that, just to see how it looks in the light. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. \n\nI just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. \n\nI suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? \n\nAnd maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your reflection or groom yourself at specific times of the day or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! \n\nIt's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. \n\nSometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your concerns about your appearance are affecting your overall happiness or well-being?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything drastic like that. I'm still a very happy person, mostly! I mean, it hasn't stopped me from going to the plant or doing my volunteer work, and I still love doing those things. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe it takes a little bit of the joy out of things sometimes? Just a smidge. Like I said, I'm a bit slower getting ready for my trainings because I'm fussing over how I look, and... and I do get a little anxious before social events now. I used to just breeze right into parties and get-togethers without a second thought, and now I hesitate. And, um, dodging the cameras when people are taking photos... it does make me feel a little silly, if I'm being honest. \n\nBut I'm still getting out there and living my life, you know? It's just a minor bump in the road. I'm sure lots of women my age feel a little less confident sometimes, right? I'd be up there, pointing things out on the diagrams, and I just couldn't help but notice how... well, how wrinkly and uneven my hands looked. And then, once that started, I began noticing my face more, especially in the company newsletter photos. I just keep thinking my face looks sort of... crooked? \n\nEven when I'm not up there teaching or looking at a picture, I'll just be sitting on the assembly line and suddenly remember how my hands looked pointing at the board. Or I'll wonder if my face looked uneven when I was talking to my supervisor. I know it sounds a bit silly, because the other ladies say they don't see what I'm talking about at all. I shouldn't be dwelling on it so much, should I? I just want to make sure I'm presenting a good image for the company. That's a good thing to care about, right?\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your hands and face affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, my family and friends are just wonderful. I really try my best not to let it cause any real problems with them. So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. \n\nIf I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. I'll look in the mirror a few times, maybe turn my face this way and that, just to see how it looks in the light. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. \n\nI just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. \n\nI suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? \n\nAnd maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your reflection or groom yourself at specific times of the day or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! \n\nIt's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. \n\nSometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your concerns about your appearance are affecting your overall happiness or well-being?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything drastic like that. I'm still a very happy person, mostly! I used to just breeze right into parties and get-togethers without a second thought, and now I hesitate. And, um, dodging the cameras when people are taking photos... it does make me feel a little silly, if I'm being honest. \n\nBut I'm still getting out there and living my life, you know? It's just a minor bump in the road. I'm sure lots of women my age feel a little less confident sometimes, right? I shouldn't be dwelling on it so much, should I? I just want to make sure I'm presenting a good image for the company. Having a stray thought here and there while your hands are busy... well, that doesn't mean I'm not doing a good job, does it? I think everyone's mind wanders just a tiny bit during a long shift.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think there's any possibility that your perception of your hands and face is not accurate?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I mean, I suppose anything is possible, right? I know the other ladies at the plant have told me they don't see what I'm seeing. And my daughters always tell me I look fine. 

But... um... when I look in the mirror, or when I see those photos... it just seems so real to me. The wrinkles on my hands just look so deep, and my face... it just looks crooked, like I said. I try not to make a big fuss about it, I really do. I don't want to be one of those people who complains all the time or seems vain. 

I guess... maybe it's just that I'm looking closer than everyone else? Or maybe I'm just being a little too hard on myself. That's a very common thing for women my age to do, isn't it? Just wanting to look our best and maybe being a bit critical? I certainly don't think I'm losing my mind or seeing things that aren't there entirely... I just want to make sure I look presentable. That's perfectly normal, right?

I just don't want you to think I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your appearance interfered with your daily activities or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing anything important. I'm still at work every day, and I still do the safety trainings, of course. And, um, I guess I have been sort of... stepping out of the way when someone brings out a camera at work, or maybe pausing for a second before saying yes to going out with the girls after our shift. Just little things, really! I still go, mostly. I just don't want to ruin anyone's pictures or make them uncomfortable if I'm looking a bit... off. \n\nIt's really nothing major, I promise. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I mean... when I look down at my hands, or when I see myself in the mirror, I really do see the wrinkles and the unevenness. It looks very real to me in the moment. \n\nBut... well, since everyone else tells me they don't see what I'm seeing, I try very hard to tell myself that maybe it is just my imagination acting up. I certainly don't want to seem like I've lost my senses or anything! So I guess... I guess I feel like they look that way, but I'm trying to accept that I'm probably just being overly critical. I try to trust what other people say more than my own eyes, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous saying that. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that. \n\nSometimes I might feel a little self-conscious, like I mentioned before, but I don't actually think *they* are staring at my hands or thinking bad things about my face. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. \n\nI just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. Just a couple of times. I wouldn't let anyone down like that. \n\nI mean, I suppose... well, I might be taking just a tiny bit longer to get ready in the mornings before a training day, just trying to make sure I look presentable. And, um, I guess I have been sort of... stepping out of the way when someone brings out a camera at work, or maybe pausing for a second before saying yes to going out with the girls after our shift. Just little things, really! I still go, mostly. I just don't want to ruin anyone's pictures or make them uncomfortable if I'm looking a bit... off. \n\nIt's really nothing major, I promise. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your hands and face actually look uneven and wrinkly, or do you just feel like they might?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a really good question. I mean... when I look down at my hands, or when I see myself in the mirror, I really do see the wrinkles and the unevenness. It looks very real to me in the moment. \n\nBut... well, since everyone else tells me they don't see what I'm seeing, I try very hard to tell myself that maybe it is just my imagination acting up. I certainly don't want to seem like I've lost my senses or anything! So I guess... I guess I feel like they look that way, but I'm trying to accept that I'm probably just being overly critical. I try to trust what other people say more than my own eyes, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous saying that. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that. \n\nSometimes I might feel a little self-conscious, like I mentioned before, but I don't actually think *they* are staring at my hands or thinking bad things about my face. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. \n\nIf I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. I'll look in the mirror a few times, maybe turn my face this way and that, just to see how it looks in the light. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. \n\nI just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. \n\nI suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? \n\nAnd maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your reflection or groom yourself at specific times of the day or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! \n\nIt's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. \n\nSometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your concerns about your appearance are affecting your overall happiness or well-being?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything drastic like that. I'm still a very happy person, mostly! I mean, it hasn't stopped me from going to the plant or doing my volunteer work, and I still love doing those things. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe it takes a little bit of the joy out of things sometimes? Just a smidge. Like I said, I'm a bit slower getting ready for my trainings because I'm fussing over how I look, and... and I do get a little anxious before social events now. I used to just breeze right into parties and get-togethers without a second thought, and now I hesitate. And, um, dodging the cameras when people are taking photos... it does make me feel a little silly, if I'm being honest. \n\nBut I'm still getting out there and living my life, you know? It's just a minor bump in the road. I'm sure lots of women my age feel a little less confident sometimes, right? It's perfectly normal to not be thrilled with getting older, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your hands and face when you're not looking at them or getting ready to be in front of people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I mean, I try not to let my mind wander too much when I'm just going about my day. I like to stay focused on my work, you know? \n\nBut, um... I guess if I'm being perfectly honest... yes, they do pop into my head sometimes. It really all started when I began helping out with those safety trainings at the plant. I shouldn't be dwelling on it so much, should I? I just want to make sure I'm presenting a good image for the company. Oh, rituals? Well, I wouldn't call them rituals, exactly. That sounds a bit... intense, doesn't it? I just have a little routine, like most people do. 

I mean, before I head into one of my volunteer workshops at the senior center, or before a class, I might just... pop into the restroom real quick. Just to check the mirror a few times, you know? I'll put a little extra lotion on my hands to smooth them out, and maybe just turn my face this way and that in the light, just to make sure the unevenness isn't too obvious. 

Sometimes, if I'm really feeling a bit unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers or friends if I look all right. Just a quick question! It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes—just a few minutes!—but I always get myself there, and I always do what I've promised to do. 

It's just a little touch-up routine to feel presentable. Taking a moment to freshen up before you see people... that's just good manners, wouldn't you agree?

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has thinking about your hands and face been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say \"distress,\" exactly. That sounds so... so dramatic, you know? I don't want to make it sound like I'm falling apart or anything. It's more just... a little bothersome. \n\nI mean, I still get my work done, and I'm still smiling and chatting with the girls on the line. It's just a quiet little worry in the back of my head that I wish I could turn off. I feel a bit silly letting it get under my skin at all, to be perfectly honest. I know I should just count my blessings and ignore it. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just don't want you to think I'm making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your appearance interfered with your daily activities or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing anything important. I'm still at work every day, and I still do the safety trainings, of course. I wouldn't let anyone down like that. \n\nI mean, I suppose... well, I might be taking just a tiny bit longer to get ready in the mornings before a training day, just trying to make sure I look presentable. And, um, I guess I have been sort of... stepping out of the way when someone brings out a camera at work, or maybe pausing for a second before saying yes to going out with the girls after our shift. Just little things, really! I still go, mostly. I just don't want to ruin anyone's pictures or make them uncomfortable if I'm looking a bit... off. \n\nIt's really nothing major, I promise. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your hands and face actually look uneven and wrinkly, or do you just feel like they might?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a really good question. I mean... when I look down at my hands, or when I see myself in the mirror, I really do see the wrinkles and the unevenness. It looks very real to me in the moment. \n\nBut... well, since everyone else tells me they don't see what I'm seeing, I try very hard to tell myself that maybe it is just my imagination acting up. I certainly don't want to seem like I've lost my senses or anything! So I guess... I guess I feel like they look that way, but I'm trying to accept that I'm probably just being overly critical. I try to trust what other people say more than my own eyes, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely ridiculous saying that. Do you think it's normal to feel a bit confused about it like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are noticing or judging you because of how your hands and face look?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think that. I mean... the girls on the line are all so sweet, and I know they wouldn't do something like that. \n\nSometimes I might feel a little self-conscious, like I mentioned before, but I don't actually think *they* are staring at my hands or thinking bad things about my face. I know everyone is busy with their own work and their own lives. I certainly don't walk around thinking people are whispering about me behind my back. That would be a rather... well, a rather uncharitable way to look at people, wouldn't it? I always try to see the good in folks. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm... well, suspicious of everyone around me or anything like that. I really do think the people I work with are wonderful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hands and face actually look fine, and it's just your perception of them that's off?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose that is very possible. I mean, I really hope that's the case! \n\nSince my friends and my husband all say they don't see what I'm seeing, I do try to tell myself that it's just my own eyes playing tricks on me. I really don't want to be making a fuss over nothing, you know? So, yes, I think it's very likely that they look just fine to everyone else, and I'm just being a bit silly about it all. \n\nI'm really trying my best to listen to what others say rather than trusting my own... well, my own skewed view of things. Does that sound like a sensible way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm thinking about this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain behaviors, like checking your reflection or grooming, in an attempt to feel better about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do a little bit of that. I really hope it doesn't make me sound terribly vain or anything. \n\nIf I have somewhere to be, like before a training class at work or... or one of the workshops at the senior center, I might pop into the ladies' room just to double-check things. I'll look in the mirror a few times, maybe turn my face this way and that, just to see how it looks in the light. And I'll usually rub some extra lotion on my hands. \n\nSometimes I might even ask one of my coworkers if I look all right, just to put my mind at ease. It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule every now and then, which I always feel just awful about, but I promise I still get myself there! I don't let it stop me from going. \n\nI just like to make sure I'm presenting myself nicely. That's a fairly normal thing to do, isn't it? I hope I'm not making too much of a fuss over it.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself checking your reflection in a mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I don't really count or anything like that. I suppose... maybe just a few times a day? Like I said, just a quick peek before I go into a meeting or a class, just to make sure I'm put together. \n\nI don't spend hours staring at myself, I promise. Goodness, who has the time for that? Between the assembly line and my volunteer work, I'm much too busy to be staring into a mirror all day. It's really just a quick check here and there. That's pretty standard for most women my age, wouldn't you say? Just wanting to look tidy?\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about your appearance ever caused you to avoid social or work-related activities altogether?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I avoid things *altogether*. I mean, I would never want to let anyone down, especially at work or the senior center. I still go to all my shifts on the assembly line and my volunteer days. \n\nI suppose... well, I might be just a little bit slower getting ready for the training classes sometimes, like I mentioned. And, um, if I'm being completely honest, I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people take photos at gatherings. I just don't want to ruin their nice pictures, you know? \n\nAnd maybe I hesitate just a tiny bit more before going to social events that I used to just breeze right into. But I always end up going! I really try not to let it get in the way of my life. That's the right way to handle it, isn't it? Just pushing through and making sure I still show up?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your reflection or groom yourself at specific times of the day or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose it is mostly tied to those specific situations I mentioned earlier. It's not an all-day sort of thing, I promise! \n\nIt's really just... right before I have to be in front of people. Like, if I'm about to lead a training class at the plant, or right before one of my workshops at the senior center starts. I'll just slip into the bathroom real quick to check the mirror, you know? Just a couple of times. I'll turn my face a bit to check the lighting, and rub a little extra lotion on my hands so they don't look too... well, so they look presentable. \n\nSometimes, if I'm feeling a little unsure, I might just quietly ask a coworker if I look okay. It's just to settle my nerves, really. I know I said it makes me run a tiny bit late sometimes, but I always make sure I get there! I never miss the class. That's the important part, right? Making sure I still fulfill my responsibilities?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your concerns about your appearance are affecting your overall happiness or well-being?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything drastic like that. I'm still a very happy person, mostly! I mean, it hasn't stopped me from going to the plant or doing my volunteer work, and I still love doing those things. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe it takes a little bit of the joy out of things sometimes? Just a smidge. Like I said, I'm a bit slower getting ready for my trainings because I'm fussing over how I look, and... and I do get a little anxious before social events now. I used to just breeze right into parties and get-togethers without a second thought, and now I hesitate. And, um, dodging the cameras when people are taking photos... it does make me feel a little silly, if I'm being honest. \n\nBut I'm still getting out there and living my life, you know? It's just a minor bump in the road. I'm sure lots of women my age feel a little less confident sometimes, right? It's perfectly normal to not be thrilled with getting older, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your hands and face when you're not looking at them or getting ready to be in front of people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I mean, I try not to let my mind wander too much when I'm just going about my day. I like to stay focused on my work, you know? \n\nBut, um... I guess if I'm being perfectly honest... yes, they do pop into my head sometimes. It really all started when I began helping out with those safety trainings at the plant. I'd be up there, pointing things out on the diagrams, and I just couldn't help but notice how... well, how wrinkly and uneven my hands looked. And then, once that started, I began noticing my face more, especially in the company newsletter photos. I just keep thinking my face looks sort of... crooked? \n\nEven when I'm not up there teaching or looking at a picture, I'll just be sitting on the assembly line and suddenly remember how my hands looked pointing at the board. Or I'll wonder if my face looked uneven when I was talking to my supervisor. I know it sounds a bit silly, because the other ladies say they don't see what I'm talking about at all. I shouldn't be dwelling on it so much, should I? I just want to make sure I'm presenting a good image for the company. That's a good thing to care about, right?\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your hands and face affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Oh, my family and friends are just wonderful. I really try my best not to let it cause any real problems with them. Like I was saying a little bit ago, it certainly hasn't stopped me from working or doing my volunteering, and I still see everyone! \n\nBut, um... I guess it does change things just a tiny bit when we're together. Like I mentioned with the cameras... if my daughters or my friends want to take a group picture at a barbecue, I've started making little excuses to duck out of the frame. Or I'll offer to be the one taking the picture instead. And... well, I do hesitate a bit more before going to our neighborhood get-togethers or family dinners, which I used to just jump right into. I just take a little longer getting ready now, making sure my hands and face look acceptable so I don't embarrass anyone. \n\nBut I always show up eventually! I'd never want to let them down or be a bad friend. And they don't seem mad at me for it. It's perfectly fine to want to look nice for the people you care about, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your hands and face when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I really try my best not to let that happen. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the thoughts might pop into my head for just a second while I'm on the assembly line, but... well, I certainly don't let it actually distract me from the work itself. I'm very careful about that. \n\nYou have to stay focused when you're working the line, you know? So if I catch myself worrying about my wrinkles or if my face is looking a bit uneven, I just sort of... push it right back out of my mind and look at the parts right in front of me. I just get right back to the task at hand. I've never made a mistake or fallen behind because I was dwelling on it, I promise you that. \n\nI really pride myself on being a good, reliable worker. Having a stray thought here and there while your hands are busy... well, that doesn't mean I'm not doing a good job, does it? I think everyone's mind wanders just a tiny bit during a long shift.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your perception of your hands and face is not accurate?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I mean, I suppose anything is possible, right? I know the other ladies at the plant have told me they don't see what I'm seeing. And my daughters always tell me I look fine. \n\nBut... um... when I look in the mirror, or when I see those photos... it just seems so real to me. The wrinkles on my hands just look so deep, and my face... it just looks crooked, like I said. I try not to make a big fuss about it, I really do. I don't want to be one of those people who complains all the time or seems vain. \n\nI guess... maybe it's just that I'm looking closer than everyone else? Or maybe I'm just being a little too hard on myself. That's a very common thing for women my age to do, isn't it? Just wanting to look our best and maybe being a bit critical? I certainly don't think I'm losing my mind or seeing things that aren't there entirely... I just want to make sure I look presentable. That's perfectly normal, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to temporarily reduce your concerns about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, rituals? Well, I wouldn't call them rituals, exactly. That sounds a bit... intense, doesn't it? I just have a little routine, like most people do. \n\nI mean, before I head into one of my volunteer workshops at the senior center, or before a class, I might just... pop into the restroom real quick. Just to check the mirror a few times, you know? I'll put a little extra lotion on my hands to smooth them out, and maybe just turn my face this way and that in the light, just to make sure the unevenness isn't too obvious. \n\nSometimes, if I'm really feeling a bit unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers or friends if I look all right. Just a quick question! It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes—just a few minutes!—but I always get myself there, and I always do what I've promised to do. \n\nIt's just a little touch-up routine to feel presentable. Taking a moment to freshen up before you see people... that's just good manners, wouldn't you agree?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I just take a little longer getting ready now, making sure my hands and face look acceptable so I don't embarrass anyone. \n\nBut I always show up eventually! I'd never want to let them down or be a bad friend. And they don't seem mad at me for it. It's perfectly fine to want to look nice for the people you care about, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your hands and face when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I really try my best not to let that happen. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the thoughts might pop into my head for just a second while I'm on the assembly line, but... well, I certainly don't let it actually distract me from the work itself. I'm very careful about that. \n\nYou have to stay focused when you're working the line, you know? So if I catch myself worrying about my wrinkles or if my face is looking a bit uneven, I just sort of... push it right back out of my mind and look at the parts right in front of me. I just get right back to the task at hand. I've never made a mistake or fallen behind because I was dwelling on it, I promise you that. \n\nI really pride myself on being a good, reliable worker. Or maybe I'm just being a little too hard on myself. That's a very common thing for women my age to do, isn't it? Just wanting to look our best and maybe being a bit critical? I certainly don't think I'm losing my mind or seeing things that aren't there entirely... I just want to make sure I look presentable. Just to check the mirror a few times, you know? I'll put a little extra lotion on my hands to smooth them out, and maybe just turn my face this way and that in the light, just to make sure the unevenness isn't too obvious. \n\nSometimes, if I'm really feeling a bit unsure, I might ask one of my coworkers or friends if I look all right. Just a quick question! It does make me run a tiny bit behind schedule sometimes—just a few minutes!—but I always get myself there, and I always do what I've promised to do. \n\nIt's just a little touch-up routine to feel presentable. I've always been a very good sleeper. I just try to leave all those little worries at the door when it's time to rest. That's a good sign, don't you think? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. I would never just cancel plans and leave people in the lurch. I still go to work at the plant every day, and I never miss my volunteer shifts. I wouldn't let my little worries stop me from fulfilling my responsibilities, I promise you that. 

But... well, I suppose... I mean, I have noticed that it takes me a little bit longer to get ready for trainings at work now. Just trying to make sure I'm put together so I don't stand out in a bad way. And... I guess I have started sort of... ducking out of the way when people bring out a camera. I just don't want my face or my hands ruining a nice picture for everyone else, you know? 

And maybe... sometimes I hesitate a little bit before going to a social event that I used to just jump right into. I just need an extra minute to talk myself into going and feeling okay about how I look. But I almost always push through and go! It's perfectly normal to be a little camera-shy or self-conscious at my age, isn't it? Since I'm still doing all the things I need to do, it's not a real problem, right?
